Disassembly: omni ivwb 2.55 u3.bin
=====================================

Source: unidentified gravimetric blender/feeder controller for plastics
        processing - NOT the dryer this project started with (confirmed
        via the on-board message table, see below). "OMNI IVWB" per the
        file's own name; exact manufacturer/model not otherwise
        identified. Provided as a second real firmware sample. See
        omni-ivwb-2.55.summary.md, omni-ivwb-2.55.protocol.md, and
        omni-ivwb-2.55.messages.md.

Architecture: Intel MCS-51 (8051-family, likely 8052 variant - Timer 2
              vector present, same as both dryer dumps)

Range disassembled: $0000-$7FFF. The physical EPROM image is 64KB, but
                     the first and second 32KB halves are byte-for-byte
                     identical (confirmed via direct comparison) - this
                     is a 32KB ROM's contents mirrored to fill a 64KB
                     part (or the board only decodes 15 address lines).
                     Only the first half is disassembled here.

Method: linear-sweep disassembly (decodes every byte in address order as
        an instruction, does not trace actual code flow) - same tool and
        limitations as the dryer disassemblies. The message table
        ($6AE2-$7406) will misdecode as bogus instructions.

Format: <address>  <raw bytes>  <mnemonic + operands>  [; inline comment]

Confirmed findings this pass:
  - Standard 8051/8052 interrupt vector table at $0000, reset at $002E
  - UART init at $0086 (SCON=$50, mode 1, 8-bit, REN=1)
  - Serial ISR at $789F implements ANSI X3.28 / SPI-CCP-family control-code
    framing: SOH=$01, STX=$02, ETX=$03, ENQ=$05 are explicitly compared
    against each received byte - see the $789F and $2869 annotations below
    and omni-ivwb-2.55.protocol.md for the full writeup.
  - A 130-entry display message table at $6AE2 (18 bytes/entry) - confirms
    this is a gravimetric blender/feeder (recipe, component, fill-rate,
    calibration terminology) - see omni-ivwb-2.55.messages.md.
  - A response-buffer builder ($3E08-$3E4C) that starts a frame with
    $02 (STX) - the field at buffer offset 3 is read from external
    memory $4CE3, which IS writable (see below) - not read-only as first
    reported.
  - A range-based dispatch table search at $47A2 (same return-address-
    trick family as the dryer's $0A28, but comparing [low,high] ranges
    per entry instead of a single match byte) - likely the command
    dispatcher, not fully decoded in this pass.
  - CONFIRMED: a U3->U4 bank-switch trampoline at $4C5E-$4C83, using
    P1.0 as the bank-select line (force low then high, jump to a
    caller-supplied DPTR now resolved against U4, return via the callee
    doing "LJMP $4C7A" which restores P1.0 and RETs). Seven fixed call
    sites use it: $0140, $1B80, $1D44, $1D58, $3D4F, $4ECA, $647B - see
    omni-ivwb-2.55.protocol.md for the full trace, including the U4
    entry point at $984A this uncovered (a power-on default initializer
    for $4CE3-$4CE6, corrected from an earlier, wrong "menu save
    routine" reading).

Not confirmed / open questions - see omni-ivwb-2.55.protocol.md:
  - The exact DEVID value (SPI-CCP terms) this device presents
  - The full CMD table and per-command data formats
  - Which of $4CE3-$4CE6 (if any) is genuinely the ADD field
  - How the user-described numbered menu system ("Menu 54: Communication
    Address Setup") maps to any of the seven known U4 entry points, or
    to some other entry point not yet located

=====================================

      ; ======================================================================
      ; MAIN LOOP: received-frame dispatch
      ; Gated on flag $25.5 (set by the RX ISR above on ETX/ENQ). Reads
      ; the first byte of the RX buffer at $8808 and branches on the same
      ; SOH/STX/ETX-or-ENQ control codes:
      ;   $01 (SOH)      -> LCALL $3D3D
      ;   $02 (STX)      -> LCALL $28B9 (below)
      ;   $03/$05 (ETX/ENQ) -> LCALL $387C
      ;   else           -> error path ($841A=5, queues a ROM message)
      ; ======================================================================

789e  32        RETI

      ; ======================================================================
      ; SERIAL (UART) INTERRUPT - RX/TX handling
      ; Reached from the Serial interrupt vector ($0023 -> $789F).
      ; On RX (SCON.0/RI set): reads SBUF, then classifies the byte against
      ; the ANSI X3.28 / SPI-CCP-family control codes - CONFIRMED by the
      ; explicit compares below (not guessed):
      ;   $01 (SOH) -> $40=$26, $42=1
      ;   $02 (STX) -> $40=$18, $42=1
      ;   $03 (ETX) -> $40=$0C, $42=0
      ;   $05 (ENQ) -> $40=$04, $42=0
      ;   else (data byte) -> $40=1, plus a status flag at $880A=$80
      ; Byte is then stored into a growing RX buffer via $3A:$3B (reset to
      ; $8808 elsewhere), with $41 counting bytes and $40 holding a
      ; per-control-code byte-count/threshold. This is the same class of
      ; block-oriented serial protocol as the dryer's SPI CCP (DLE/SOH/
      ; STX/ETX/ENQ framing) - see omni-ivwb-2.55.protocol.md.
      ; On TX (SCON.1/TI set): walks a TX buffer via $3D:$3E (reset to
      ; $882E after each completed send) and writes each byte to SBUF.
      ; ======================================================================
